# ​D23.3.1 Instructions for accessing non-debug System registers

Source: <https://developer.arm.com/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D23-AArch64-System-Register-Encoding/-D23-3-Moves-to-and-from-non-debug-System-registers--Special-purpose-registers/-D23-3-1-Instructions-for-accessing-non-debug-System-registers>

##### D23.3.1 Instructions for accessing non-debug System registers

The A64 instructions for accessing System registers are:

```
    MSR <System_register>, Xt     ; Write to 64-bit System register
    MSRR <System_register>, Xt    ; Write to 128-bit System register
    MRS Xt, <System_register>     ; Read from 64-bit System register
    MRRS Xt, <System_register>    ; Read from 128-bit System register
```

Where `<System_register>` is the register name, for example [MIDR\_EL1](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-136-MIDR-EL1--Main-ID-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_midr_el1).

This section includes only the System register access encodings for which both:

- `op0` is `0b11`.
- The value of `CRn` is one of {`0b0000`, `0b0001`, `0b0010`, `0b0011`, `0b0100`, `0b0101`, `0b0110`, `0b0111`, `0b1001`, `0b1010`, `0b1100`, `0b1101`, `0b1110`}.

> #### Note
>
> - These encodings access the registers that are equivalent to the AArch32 System registers in the (`coproc` == `0b1111`) encoding space. For more information, see [Mapping of the System registers between the Execution states](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D1-The-AArch64-System-Level-Programmers--Model/-D1-10-Interprocessing/-D1-10-1-Register-mappings-between-AArch32-state-and-AArch64-state?lang=en#beifbcjb).
> - While this group is described as accessing the non-debug System registers, its correct characterization is by the {`op0`, `CRn`} values given in this subsection, and the group includes the debug System registers [DLR\_EL0](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers/-D24-3-13-DLR-EL0--Debug-Link-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_dlr_el0), [DSPSR\_EL0](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers/-D24-3-14-DSPSR-EL0--Debug-Saved-Program-Status-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_dspsr_el0), [MDCR\_EL2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers/-D24-3-17-MDCR-EL2--Monitor-Debug-Configuration-Register--EL2-?lang=en#reg_aarch64_mdcr_el2), [MDCR\_EL3](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers/-D24-3-18-MDCR-EL3--Monitor-Debug-Configuration-Register--EL3-?lang=en#reg_aarch64_mdcr_el3), and [SDER32\_EL3](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers/-D24-3-30-SDER32-EL3--AArch32-Secure-Debug-Enable-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_sder32_el3), which are described in [Debug System registers](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-3-Debug-System-registers?lang=en#aarch64_debug_system_registers). These registers are exceptions to the standard encoding of debug System registers, which has `op0` == `0b10`. See [Instructions for accessing debug System registers](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D23-AArch64-System-Register-Encoding/-D23-2-Moves-to-and-from-debug-and-trace-System-registers/-D23-2-1-Instructions-for-accessing-debug-System-registers?lang=en#babdfgfi) for more information.

Direct accesses to some regions of the System register encoding space have a defined behavior even if no register is currently allocated or implemented for the encodings in those regions. The Feature ID space is the System register space in AArch64 with `op0==3, op1=={0, 1, 3}, CRn==0, CRm=={0-7}, op2=={0-7}`. The properties for this space are that:

- For unused encodings in the part of the space that has `op1` == `0` and `CRm` == `{2-7}`, the following apply:
  - If [FEAT\_FGT](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-7-The-Armv8-6-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_fgt) is not implemented, it is IMPLEMENTATION DEFINED whether direct reads can be trapped by [HCR\_EL2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-61-HCR-EL2--Hypervisor-Configuration-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_hcr_el2).TID3.
  - If [FEAT\_FGT](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-7-The-Armv8-6-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_fgt) is implemented, direct reads can be trapped by [HCR\_EL2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-61-HCR-EL2--Hypervisor-Configuration-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_hcr_el2).TID3.
  - If [FEAT\_IDTE3](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-3-Armv9-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-3-7-The-Armv9-6-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_idte3) is implemented, direct reads can be trapped by [SCR\_EL3](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-168-SCR-EL3--Secure-Configuration-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_scr_el3).TID3.
  - If [FEAT\_IDST](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-5-The-Armv8-4-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_idst) is not implemented, direct reads from EL0 are UNDEFINED.
  - If [FEAT\_IDST](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-5-The-Armv8-4-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_idst) is implemented, direct reads from EL0 are trapped to EL1 if [HCR\_EL2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-61-HCR-EL2--Hypervisor-Configuration-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_hcr_el2).TGE is 0, or to EL2 if [HCR\_EL2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D24-AArch64-System-Register-Descriptions/-D24-2-General-system-control-registers/-D24-2-61-HCR-EL2--Hypervisor-Configuration-Register?lang=en#reg_aarch64_hcr_el2).TGE is 1.
  - Direct reads from EL1 or above that are not trapped behave as Reserved, RES0 to ensure correct behavior if the encodings are used for ID registers in the future.
- For unused encodings in the part of the space that has either `op1` == `0` and `CRm` == `0`, or `op1` == `{1, 3}`, the following apply:
  - If [FEAT\_IDST](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-5-The-Armv8-4-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_idst) is not implemented, direct reads are UNDEFINED and therefore generate exceptions reported with EC syndrome value `0x00`.
  - If [FEAT\_IDST](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-A-Arm-Architecture-Introduction-and-Overview/-Chapter-A2-A-profile-Architecture-Extensions/-A2-2-Armv8-A-architecture-extensions/-A2-2-5-The-Armv8-4-architecture-extension?lang=en#feat_feat_idst) is implemented, direct reads generate exceptions reported with EC syndrome value `0x18`.
  - The behavior for direct reads is consistent with the pseudocode function [`UnimplementedIDRegister`](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-J-Architectural-Pseudocode/-Chapter-J1-A-profile-Architecture-Pseudocode/-J1-4-Shared-pseudocode/-J1-4-496-UnimplementedIDRegister?lang=en#asl_func_unimplementedidregister_0)`()`.

The instruction encoding for these accesses is:

![](images/4885-D23.3.1-Instructions-for-accessing-non-debug-System-registers-img01.svg)

[Table D23-2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D23-AArch64-System-Register-Encoding/-D23-3-Moves-to-and-from-non-debug-System-registers--Special-purpose-registers/-D23-3-1-Instructions-for-accessing-non-debug-System-registers?lang=en#tbl_aarch64_register_encoding_table_mrsmsr_mrrsmsrr_op011) shows the encodings of the register access instructions.

See the register descriptions for information about the control that determines whether these accesses are permitted.

###### D23.3.1.1 About the GIC System registers

From version 3 of the GIC architecture, the specification defines three groups of System registers, identified by the prefix of the register name:

ICC\_
:   GIC physical CPU interface System registers.

ICH\_
:   GIC virtual interface control System registers.

ICV\_
:   GIC Virtual CPU interface System registers.

> #### Note
>
> These registers are in addition to the GIC memory-mapped register groups GICC\_, GICD\_, GICH\_, GICR\_, GICV\_, and GITS\_.

When implemented, the GIC System registers form part of an Arm processor implementation, and therefore these registers are included in the register summaries. However, the registers are defined only in the GIC Architecture Specification.

As [Table D23-2](/documentation/ddi0487/mc/-Part-D-The-AArch64-System-Level-Architecture/-Chapter-D23-AArch64-System-Register-Encoding/-D23-3-Moves-to-and-from-non-debug-System-registers--Special-purpose-registers/-D23-3-1-Instructions-for-accessing-non-debug-System-registers?lang=en#tbl_aarch64_register_encoding_table_mrsmsr_mrrsmsrr_op011) shows, the ICV\_\* registers have the same {`op0`, `op1`, `CRn`, `CRm`, `op2`} encodings as the corresponding ICC\_\* registers. For these encodings, GIC register configuration fields determine which register is accessed.

For more information, see the *Arm® Generic Interrupt Controller Architecture Specification, GIC architecture version 3 and version 4* (ARM IHI 0069).
